BE-2 BODY ELECTRICAL – LIGHTING
BODY ELECTRICAL
LIGHTING
(cid:1)HEADLIGHT
(cid:2) The multi-reflector type headlights are used as standard equipment.
(cid:2) With the multi-reflector type headlights, the light from the bulbs is dispersed and distributed through
multiple parabolic shaped reflectors. As a result, the lens cut pattern is no longer provided in the center
of the lens, thus realizing a clear look.
Multi-reflector
271BE01
(cid:3) Light Distribution Imaginary Diagram (cid:4)

(Multi Parabolic Shape)
Headlight Bulb
Lens

---

BODY ELECTRICAL – LIGHTING BE-3
(cid:1)LIGHT REMINDER SYSTEM
(cid:2) This system is used as standard equipment on the SR grade model.
(cid:2) This system sounds the buzzer in the combination meter to warn the driver that the lights remain ON if
the driver’s door is opened while the headlights or taillights are ON when the ignition key is turned from
the ON to ACC or LOCK positions. The buzzer is located inside the combination meter.
(cid:2) This system is controlled by the meter ECU.

BE-10 BODY ELECTRICAL – METER
4. Fuel Filter Warning Light (Diesel Engine Model)
General
(cid:2) In addition to the conventional fuel sedimenter warning functions, this light illuminates to inform the
driver of a failure in case the clogging of the fuel filter is detected from the fuel filter warning switch.
(cid:2) The illumination conditions of this light vary depending on the functions indicated below.
Function Failure Activation
Fuel Filter Warning Illumination
Fuel Sedimenter Warning Blinking
(cid:2) If a failure is detected simultaneously from the fuel sedimenter level switch and the fuel filter warning
switch, the system gives precedence to the fuel sedimenter warning and causes the warning light to blink.
(cid:2) This system is controlled by meter ECU.
(cid:3) System Diagram (cid:4)
Combination Meter
Vehicle Speed Sensor
Fuel Sedimenter Level
Warning Switch
Meter
ECU
Fuel Filter Warning
Fuel Filter
Switch
Warning Light
Ignition Switch
271BE59
Operation
1) Fuel Sedimenter Warning
When the amount of water that has accumulated in the sedimenter reaches a predetermined level, the fuel
sedimenter level switch, which is located in the bottom of the fuel filter, turns ON. The meter ECU
renders this signal as a fuel sedimenter warning and causes the fuel filter warning light to blink.
2) Fuel Filter Warning
(cid:2) The fuel filter warning switch is located in the upper area of the fuel filter.
(cid:2) To prevent false alarms on a cold engine, the meter ECU starts its judgment function with the ignition
switch turned ON, after a total of 30 minutes have elapsed with the input of vehicle speed signal
information (minimum vehicle speed of 2.5 km/h).
(cid:2) The fuel filter warning switch turns OFF when the vacuum in the fuel filter reaches a predetermined
level.
(cid:2) The meter ECU illuminates the fuel filter warning light when the OFF signal from the fuel filter
warning switch meets either of the following conditions:
– OFF signals from the fuel filter warning switch are input continuously for 10 minutes.
– When the ignition switch is in the ON position, OFF signal (between 1 second and 10 minutes in
duration) from the fuel filter warning switch is input twice with a minimum interval of one second.

BODY ELECTRICAL – METER BE-11
Service Tip
When the fuel filter warning switch detects a failure, the meter ECU stores this failure in memory,
and continues to illuminate* the fuel filter warning light. Therefore, after replacing the fuel filter,
the illumination information stored in the meter ECU memory must be erased through the
following procedure:
1. With the ignition switch turned OFF, disconnect the fuel filter warning switch connector.
2. Turn the ignition switch from OFF to ON.
3. After more than 3 seconds but less than 60 seconds have elapsed, reconnect the connector.
4. After approximately 3 seconds have elapsed with the fuel filter warning switch closed, the
system confirms the deletion of the illumination information by turning OFF* the warning
light.
*: The warning light will blink in case of a sedimenter failure.

BE-12 BODY ELECTRICAL – COMBINATION SWITCH
COMBINATION SWITCH
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
A combination switch consisting of a lightweight and compact wiper/washer switch and a light control
switch has been adopted. On the previous model, a bracket was used to mount the combination switch to the
steering column. On the new model, this has been changed so that the combination switch is mounted directly
onto the steering column with a band, resulting in weight reduction.

BODY ELECTRICAL – AIR CONDITIONER BE-21
(cid:1)CONSTRUCTION AND OPERATION
1. Heater Control Panel
General
(cid:2) The rotary switch type heater control panel is used.
(cid:2) Conventionally, the air outlet mode and the air inlet temperature control switches were operated with a
single cable. Instead, the new model has adopted a method that uses two cables on pulleys that are always
operated in the pulling direction.
(cid:2) Five air outlet modes are provided on the control panel with a heater. To enable finer mode settings, a
positive feel is provided between the positions of these modes, thus enhancing the comfort level.

BE-22 BODY ELECTRICAL – AIR CONDITIONER
Heater Control Cable
(cid:2) Conventionally, the air outlet mode and the air inlet temperature control switches were operated with a
single cable. Instead, the new model has adopted a method that uses two cables on pulleys that are always
operated in the pulling direction.
(cid:2) This cable is circular, and is placed around the cable pulleys that are provided at the heater control panel
and the damper.
(cid:2) The operation of the heater control panel is transmitted to the damper via the control cable, which always
moves in the pulling direction. Due to the consistent action point of the pulleys, the fluctuation of the
operating effort has been minimized through the use of the pulleys. These measures have improved the
ease of use and reduced the operating effort.

2. Air conditioner Unit
General
A semi-center location air conditioner unit, in which the evaporator and heater core are placed in the
vehicle’s longitudinal direction. As a result, the air conditioner unit has been made compact and lightweight.

a) The heat exchanging efficiency has been improved.

c) The evaporator has been made thinner.
(cid:2) The evaporator body has been coated with a type of resin that contains an antibacterial agent in order to
minimize the source of foul odor and the propagation of bacteria. The substrate below this coating

3. Compressor
A compact, lightweight, and low-noise 10-cylinder swash plate type compressor has been adopted.

271BE25
4. Condenser
General
The new Hilux has adopted a sub-cool condenser. This is a multi-flow condenser consisting of two cooling
portions: a condensing portion and a super-cooling portion, and a gas-liquid separator (modulator) all
integrated together. This condenser has adopted a sub-cool cycle for its cooling cycle system to improve
heat-exchanging efficiency.
Sub-Cool Cycle
In the sub-cool cycle, after the refrigerant passes through the condensing portion of the condenser, both the
liquid refrigerant and the gaseous refrigerant that could not be liquefied, are cooled again in the
super-cooling portion. Thus, the refrigerant is sent to the evaporator in an almost completely liquefied state.
Multi-Flow Condenser Condensing Portion
Gaseous Refrigerant
Modulator
Liquid Refrigerant
Super-Cooling Portion
271BE26

BODY ELECTRICAL – AIR CONDITIONER BE-27
NOTE (Except Central and South American Models):
The point at which the air bubbles disappear in the refrigerant of the sub-cool cycle is lower than the proper
amount of refrigerant with which the system must be filled. Therefore, if the system is recharged with
refrigerant based on the point at which the air bubbles disappear, the amount of refrigerant would be
insufficient. As a result, the cooling performance of the system will be affected. If the system is overcharged
with refrigerant, this will also lead to a reduced performance.
For the proper method of verifying the amount of the refrigerant and to recharge the system with refrigerant,
see the Hilux Repair Manual.

the refrigerant volume by measuring the refrigerant pressure with a refrigerant charging gauge. For

BE-28 BODY ELECTRICAL – MULTI-INFORMATION DISPLAY
MULTI-INFORMATION DISPLAY
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) The multi-information display has been adopted as optional equipment on Australian SR grade models.
(cid:2) The display functions of this system can be switched by pressing a switch on the display panel.
(cid:2) The following pieces of information appear on the multi-information display: clock, ambient temperature,
average fuel consumption, instant fuel consumption, average vehicle speed, elapsed time, drivable
distance, and compass. The ECU, which is built into the display, causes these pieces of information to
appear on the display in accordance with the information signals provided by the meter ECU and sensors.

Switch (cid:2)

(cid:2)

(cid:2) In the average fuel consumption mode or instant fuel consumption mode, pressing this
switch for 2.3 seconds or more causes the display unit to flash, enabling the unit to be
changed.
(cid:2) In the compass mode, pressing this switch for 2.3 seconds or more causes the
multi-information display to transfer to the compass correction mode.
(cid:2) In the average fuel consumption mode, average vehicle speed mode, or elapsed time
mode, pressing this switch for 0.8 seconds or more resets the display contents of the
respective mode.
(cid:2) When the unit is flashing as a result of operating the INFO switch in the average fuel
RESET consumption mode or the instant fuel consumption mode, pressing this switch causes the
Switch display unit to change.
(cid:2) In the compass correction mode, pressing this switch causes the azimuth display portion
to flash and transfer to the turning correction mode. Within 2 minutes following the
transfer to this mode, the vehicle must be turned (360(cid:7) minimum) to automatically
complete the azimuth correction.

(cid:2) This system is a convenient system for locking and unlocking all the doors, at a distance.
(cid:2) A key integrated, two buttons type or three buttons type*1 transmitter is used.
(cid:2) The functions of the wireless door lock remote control system on the Hilux differ between the models with
and without the theft deterrent system.
(cid:2) On the model with the theft deterrent system, the wireless door control receiver is integrated in the theft
deterrent ECU.
(cid:2) The wireless door lock receiver performs the code identification process and the integration relay (the
body ECU) effect the door lock control.
(cid:2) On the model without the theft deterrent system, a serial data link is provided for establishing
communication between the wireless door control receiver and the integration relay.

BODY ELECTRICAL – WIRELESS DOOR LOCK REMOTE CONTROL BE-39
2. Transmitter Recognition Code Registration Function
Four different codes can be rewritten through the use of the registration function modes described in the table
below. The codes are electronically registered (written to and stored) in the EEPROM. To register the codes
correctly, refer to the detailed recognition code registration procedure in the Hilux Repair Manual.
Mode Function
Erases all previously registered codes and registers only the newly
Rewrite Mode received codes. This mode is used whenever a transmitter, the Body
ECU*1 or the theft deterrent ECU*2 is replaced.
Adds a newly received code while preserving any previously registered
Add Mode codes.This mode is used when adding a new transmitter. If the number
of codes exceeds 4, the oldest registered code is erased first.
Confirms how many codes are currently registered. When adding a
Confirm Mode*1
new code, this mode is used to check how many codes already exist.
To delete all the registered codes and to prohibit the wireless door lock
Prohibit Mode
function.This mode is used when the transmitter is lost.
*1: Only for Models without Theft Deterrent System
*2: Only for Models with Theft Deterrent System
BE

BE-40 BODY ELECTRICAL – ENGINE IMMOBILIZER SYSTEM
ENGINE IMMOBILIZER SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) The engine immobilizer system is theft deterrent system, which disables the engine from starting using
the ignition key without an ID code that matches the pre-registered code in the vehicle.
(cid:2) This system consists of the transponder chip, coil, amplifier, and transponder key ECU. This system adopts
a transponder system, which uses a transponder chip embedded in the grip of the ignition key. When the
coil located around the ignition key cylinder receives the ID code signal transmitted by the transponder
chip, the engine ECU determines whether or not the ID code matches the code stored.

BODY ELECTRICAL – THEFT DETERRENT SYSTEM BE-41
THEFT DETERRENT SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) This system has been adopted as standard or optional equipment on the models for Thailand, the
Philippines, and Central and South America.
(cid:2) The theft deterrent system utilizes the components of the door lock control system and the wireless door
lock remote control system. It activates when somebody attempts to forcibly enter the vehicle, open the
engine hood, or apply abnormal vibration to the vehicle, or when the battery terminals are removed and
reconnected.
(cid:2) The shock sensing triggering sensor is built in the theft deterrent ECU.
(cid:2) The control of this system is effected by the theft deterrent ECU.
(cid:2) The warning specifications of this system are listed below:

BODY ELECTRICAL – THEFT DETERRENT SYSTEM BE-43
(cid:1)SYSTEM OPERATION
1. General
(cid:2) The theft deterrent ECU effects control of the system. If it detects an abnormal in the vehicle based on an
activation signal from one of the sensor or switches, it issues a warning by operating the hazard light,
security horn and the vehicle horn.
(cid:2) The table below describes the operation of the theft deterrent system.
Function Operation
The theft deterrent system activates and deactivates by operating the
Remote arming/disarming
LOCK/UNLOCK switch on the transmitter.
If the doors are unlocked through the operation of the UNLOCK switch
on the transmitter while the system is in the armed state, the automatic
Automatic arming
lock function will lock the doors 30 seconds later and, at the same time,
activate the theft deterrent system automatically.
The theft deterrent ECU has a built-in shock sensing triggering sensor
to detect an abnormal vibration that is applied to the vehicle, and issues
an alarm only if a strong impact is applied to the vehicle. Even if the
sensor detects a vibration, the system will not issue an alarm under the
The shock sensing triggering following conditions:
sensor function (cid:2) Any door or the engine hood is open
(cid:2) Within 3 seconds after all doors are closed or the engine hood is
closed

(cid:2) Ignition switch is ON

BE-46 BODY ELECTRICAL – SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) The Hilux has adopted a driver SRS (Supplementary Restraint System) airbag and driver and front
passenger SRS airbags. The provision of these airbags varies by destination and grade model.
(cid:2) The SRS airbag helps reduce the shocks to the heads and chests of the driver and front passenger*1 in the
event of severe frontal impact collision as supplements to the seat belts.
(cid:2) On the gasoline engine model, the airbag sensor assembly includes a fuel cut control that stops the fuel
pump when an airbag is deployed.

BE-48 BODY ELECTRICAL – SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
(cid:1)SYSTEM OPERATION
(cid:2) In conjunction with impact absorbing structure for front collision, the driver and passenger*1 SRS airbags
have been designed to help reduce the impact to the head and chest in the event of a severe front collision.
These airbags are supplements to the seat belts.
(cid:2) The deceleration sensor is enclosed in the front airbag sensor. Based on the deceleration of the vehicle
during a front collision, a distortion is created in the sensor and converted into an electrical signal.
Accordingly, the extent of the initial collision can be detected in detail.

Airbag*1

*2: Only for Australian Model
(cid:1)DIAGNOSIS
General
(cid:2) If the airbag sensor assembly detects a malfunction in the SRS airbag system, the airbag sensor assembly
stores the malfunction data in memory, in addition to illuminating the SRS warning light.
(cid:2) The DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) can be read by connecting the SST (09843-18040) to the Tc and
CG terminals of the DLC3, and observing the blinking of the SRS warning light (2-digit code), or by
connecting an intelligent tester II (5-digit code). For details, see the Hilux Repair Manual.
(cid:2) If the SRS Airbag deploys, the airbag sensor assembly will turn on the SRS airbag warning light. However,
different from the ordinary diagnosis function, a DTC will not be memorized. The airbag warning light
can be turned off only by changing the airbag sensor assembly to a new one.

BODY ELECTRICAL – SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM BE-49
Service Tip
DTC Clearance (Using not intelligent tester II)
The DTCs are cleared by opening and shorting the Tc and CG (or ground) terminals of the DLC3 in
accordance with the timing chart shown below.
1)Using SST (09843-18040), connect terminal TC and CG terminals of the DLC3.
2)Disconnect terminal TC of DLC3 within 10 seconds after the DTCs appear, and check if the SRS
warning light come on within 3 seconds.
3)Within 2 seconds to 4 seconds after the SRS warning light come on, reconnect the Tc and CG
terminals of the DLC3.
4)SRS warning light goes off 3 seconds after reconnecting the Tc and CG terminals of the DLC3.
Then disconnect the terminal TC of the DLC3 within 2 to 4 seconds after the SRS warning light
goes off.

BE-50 BODY ELECTRICAL – CRUISE CONTROL SYSTEAAM
CRUISE CONTROL SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) Once the system is set to a desired vehicle speed, the throttle valve position or injection volume is adjusted
automatically to maintain the vehicle speed without depressing the accelerator pedal.
(cid:2) On the models with the 1GR-FE engine, this system effects control through the ETCS-i.
(cid:2) On the models with the 1KD-FTV engine, this system effects control through the diesel EFI system.
(cid:2) The cruise control ECU is integrated in the engine ECU.
(cid:2) This system is standard equipment on the SR grade model equipped with automatic transmission for
Australia and Central and South America.

BE-52 BODY ELECTRICAL – CRUISE CONTROL SYSTEAAM
(cid:1)FUNCTION
The cruise control has the following functions.
Function Outline
The engine ECU compares the actual vehicle speed and the set speed and if the
1GR-FE vehicle speed is higher than the set speed, it activates the throttle control motor in
Engine the throttle closing direction. If the vehicle speed is lower than the set speed, it
Constant
activates the throttle motor in the throttle opening direction.
SSppeeeedd
The engine ECU compares the actual vehicle speed and the set speed. If the vehicle
Control 1KD-
speed is higher than the set speed, the engine ECU decreases the injection volume
FTV
by regulating the EDU. If the vehicle speed is lower than the set speed, the engine
Engine
ECU increases the injection volume by regulating the EDU.
When the cruise control switch is pressed to the SET/ – side and released while the vehicle is
Set Control running within a cruising speed control range [approx. 40 km/h (25 mph) or more] with the MAIN
switch turned on, the engine ECU stores the vehicle speed and controls it at that speed constantly.
The low speed limit is the lowest speed that cruise control can be set and is designed at approx. 40
km/h (25 mph). The cruise control cannot be set below that speed. If the vehicle speed drops below
Low Speed
that speed while running in cruise control mode, the cruise control is cancelled automatically. At
Limit Control
this time, the set speed is held in the memory on the 1GR-FE engine model, and deleted on the
1KD-FTV engine model.
When the driver keeps the cruise control switch pushed to the SET/ + side while the vehicle is
running in cruise control mode, the system accelerates the engine in the following manner: on the
1GR-FE engine model, the system actuates the throttle control motor in the direction to open the
Accelerator
throttle; and on the 1KD-FTV engine model, the system controls the EDU to increase the injection
Control
volume. As the vehicle continues to accelerate, the engine ECU stores the vehicle speed at time the
driver’s hand is released from the cruise control switch. From then on, the engine ECUcontrols the
vehicle at that speed.
When the difference between the actual vehicle speed and the set speed is less than 5 km/h (3 mph),
Tap-Up
the set speed can be increased approx. 1.6 km/h (1 mph) each time by operating the RES/+ switch
Control
quickly within approx. 0.6 seconds.
When the cruise control switch is kept pushed to the SET/ – side while running in
cruise control mode, the throttle control motor is energized in the throttle closing
1GR-FE
direction. The vehicle keeps decelerating the engine ECU stores the vehicle speed
Engine
when the hand is released from the cruise control switch. From then on, the engine
ECU controls the vehicle speed at that speed constantly
CCooaasstt CCoonnttrrool
When the cruise control switch is kept pushed to the SET/ – side while the vehicle
1KD- is running in cruise control mode, the engine ECU decreases the injection volume
FTV by regulating the EDU. As the vehicle continues to decelerate, the engine ECU stores
Engine the vehicle speed at the time the driver’s hand is released from the cruise control
switch. From then on, the engine ECU continues to control the vehicle at that speed.
When the difference between the actual vehicle speed and the set speed is less than 5 km/h (3 mph),
Tap-Down the set speed can be lowered approx. 1.6 km/h (1 mph) each time by operating the SET/ – switch
quickly within approx. 0.6 seconds
After cruise control mode is cancelled by any of the cancel switches, that mode can be resumed and
controlled at the set speed by operating the cruise control switch in the RES/ + direction providing
that the vehicle speed has not dropped below the low speed limit [approx. 40 km/h (25 mph)].
(cid:2) On the 1GR-FE engine model, if the vehicle speed is below the low speed limit, the system will
Resume
not delete the speed in the memory, allowing cruise control mode to be resumed once the vehicle
Control
speed reaches above the low speed limit.
(cid:2) On the 1KD-FTV engine model, if the vehicle speed has dropped below the low speed limit, the
system will delete the speed in the memory. Therefore, cruise control mode cannot be resumed
until the cruise control is set to a vehicle speed that is higher than the low speed limit.
(Continued)

BODY ELECTRICAL – CRUISE CONTROL SYSTEAAM BE-53
Function Outline
If any of the following signals is sent to the engine ECU while the vehicle is running in cruise control
mode, cruise control mode is cancelled accordingly.
Manual (cid:2) Stop light switch ON signal (Depress the brake pedal)
Cancel (cid:2) D position circuit in neutral start switch OFF signal (Shift the transmission shift lever from D
Control to N, 2, or L)
(cid:2) CANCEL switch ON signal
(cid:2) MAIN switch OFF signal
When any of the following conditions occur during cruise control driving, the speed that is set in
the memory is cleared to cancel cruise control mode. Furthermore, the cruise main indicator light
blinks until the MAIN switch is turned OFF, and the operation of the cruise control is disabled until
the MAIN switch is turned ON again.
(cid:2) Stop light switch open or short circuit
(cid:2) The vehicle speed signal abnormal
(cid:2) ETCS-i malfunction*1
(cid:2) Common-rail fuel injection system abnormal*2
When the following condition occur during the cruise control driving, the speed that is set in the
memory is cleared to cancel cruise control mode. Furthermore, the cruise main indicator light blinks
until the MAIN switch is turned OFF, and the operation of the cruise control is disabled until the
ignition switch is turned OFF again.
(cid:2) Stop light switch input signal abnormal
Automatic (cid:2) Cancel circuit abnormal
Cancel When any of the following conditions occurs during the cruise control driving, cruise
Control control is cancelled. (Cruise control can be restarted)
(cid:2) The vehicle speed falls below the low speed limit [approx. 40 km/h (25 mph)]
1GR-FE (The set speed remains stored in the memory so that cruise control can be resumed
Engine at the set speed by operating the RESUME switch.)
(cid:2) The vehicle speed falls by 16 km/h (25 mph) or more from the set speed (The set
speed is cleared from the memory. The cruise control driving speed is needed to
be set again.) BE
When any of the following conditions occurs during the cruise control driving, the
set speed in the memory is cleared to cancel cruise control mode. Cruise control can
be resumed at the set speed by operating the SET or RESUME switch providing that
1KD-FTV
the vehicle speed is above the lower speed limit [approx. 40 km/h (25 mph)].
Engine
(cid:2) The vehicle speed falls below the low speed limit [approx. 40 km/h (25 mph)]
(cid:2) The vehicle speed drops more than 16 km/h (10 mph) below the set speed as in
uphill driving.
When the vehicle is cruising uphill, there is a case where the overdrive turns off depending on the ECT
Automatic
control. After that, when the engine ECU judges the end of cruising up from the throttle valve opening
Transmission
angle, the overdrive will turn on again after about 3 seconds. Also, in case that the overdrive turns off
Control
during accelerator or resume control, it will turns on after finishing accelerator or resume control.
When the engine ECU does not receive a vehicle speed signal for a predetermined period of time
during cruising, or when cruise control is cancelled (automatic cancel) by a malfunction in the
Diagnosis cruise control switch, stop light switch or vehicle speed signal, the engine immediately blinks the
cruise main indicator light on the combination meter on and off five times to alert the driver of the
system malfunction. The contents of malfunctions are coded and stored in the engine ECU.
*1: Only for Models with 1GR-FE Engine
*2: Only for Models with 1KD-FTV Engine

BE-54 BODY ELECTRICAL – CRUISE CONTROL SYSTEAAM
(cid:1)DIAGNOSIS
(cid:2) When the engine ECU does not receive a vehicle speed signal for a predetermined period of time during
cruising, or when cruise control is cancelled (automatic cancel) by a malfunction in the cruise control
switch, stop light switch or vehicle speed signal, the engine ECU immediately blinks the cruise main
indicator light on the combination meter on and off five times to alert the driver of the system malfunction.
The contents of malfunctions are coded and stored in the engine ECU.
(cid:2) The DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) can be accessed by connecting an intelligent tester II to the DLC3
terminal or the SST (09843-18040) to the Tc and CG terminals of the DLC3 and counting the blinking of
the cruise main indicator light.
(cid:2) An intelligent tester II can be used to read 5-digit codes. With the SST, 2-digit codes can be read from the
number of blinks of the cruise main indicator light. For details, see the Hilux Repair Manual.
(cid:2) The DTC listed below is used.

BODY ELECTRICAL – SEAT BELT REMINDER SYSTEM BE-55
SEAT BELT REMINDER SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) A seat belt reminder system is used. If the seat belt is not buckled, the reminder light flashes and the buzzer
sounds.
(cid:2) When the ignition switch is turned ON, this system determines whether or not the seat belt is buckled
depending on whether the signal from the switch in the seat belt buckle is ON or OFF.
(cid:2) The occupant detection sensor provided in the seat cushion of the front passenger seat determines whether
or not an occupant is seated in the front passenger seat.
(cid:2) On the Australian model, the buzzer sounds when the vehicle speed exceeds 15 km/h (9.3 mph) to alert
the driver that the driver seat belt is unbuckled. After 30 seconds, the seat reminder system emphasizes
the alert by changing the cycle of the buzzer for 90 seconds.
(cid:2) The specifications of this system vary by destination as indicated in the table below.

BE-56 BODY ELECTRICAL – SEAT BELT REMINDER SYSTEM
(cid:1)OCCUPANT DETECTION SENSOR
The occupant detection sensor, which is enclosed in the seat cushion of the front passenger seat, is used to
detect whether or not the front passenger seat is occupied.
This sensor, which is shaped as illustrated below, consists of a construction in which two sheets of electrodes
sandwich a spacer. When the occupant is seated, the electrode sheets come in contact with each other through
the hole that is provided in the spacer portion, thus enabling the current to flow.
Thus, the sensor detects whether or not an occupant is seated in the front passenger seat.

BODY – BODY STRUCTURE BO-3
(cid:1)SAFETY FEATURES
1. General
The impact absorbing structure of the new Hilux can help absorb the energy of impact in the event of a front
or side collision. This structure also realizes high-performance occupant protection through the use of
reinforcements that help minimize cabin deformation.
2. Impact Absorbing Structure for Frontal Collision
Frame
The brace has been added to the lower front portion of the frame.
In the event of a frontal collision, the impact energy is transmitted to the No. 1 cross member through the
brace. Thus, the moment of the backward rotation at the No. 1 cross member occurs in combination with
the moment of the forward rotation at the front suspension tower. This helps minimize the floor deformation
caused by the front suspension tower collapsing.
Floor Panel
BO
Front Impact
Energy
Brace
271BO15
Cabin
The optimally allocated panels and reinforcements allow the front impact energy received from the front
end and frame to be dissipated and absorbed by the front pillar, rocker, and floor reinforcement, in order
to minimize the deformation of the cabin.
Front Impact
Energy
271BO02
Double Cab Model

BO-4 BODY – BODY STRUCTURE
3. Impact Absorbing Structure for Side Collision
(cid:2) Impact energy of a side collision directed to the cabin area is dispersed throughout the body via pillar
reinforcements, side impact protection beam, and floor cross member.
(cid:2) This dispersion of energy helps keep the energy directed to the cabin to minimum level.
Side Impact
Energy
271BO03

BODY – BODY STRUCTURE BO-5
(cid:2) A Head Impact Protection Structure is used. With this type of construction, if the occupant’s head hits
against the roof side rail and pillar in reaction to a collision, the inner ribs of the roof side rail and pillar
collapses to help reduce the impact.

CH-2 CHASSIS – CLUTCH
CHASSIS
CLUTCH
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
The new Hilux has a clutch system with the following features.
(cid:2) A dry, single-plate clutch operated by hydraulic pressure is used, which is matched to the performance of
each engine.
(cid:2) A clutch master cylinder made of plastic has been adopted on except 1GR-FE engine models.
(cid:2) A clutch disc that uses an organic solvent-free material has been adopted to help protect the environment.
In addition, the components of the clutch damper have been optimized to realize wear resistance and
reduce noise and vibration.
(cid:2) A clutch accumulator is used to reduce the noise and vibration on the 1KD-FTV, 2KD-FTV, 2KD-FTV
High Version and 1GR-FE engine models.
(cid:2) A turnover mechanism, which reduces clutch pedal effort, is used on the 1KD-FTV, 2KD-FTV High
Version and 1GR-FE engine models.

CH-10 CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) The new Hilux has been adopted the A340E, A340F, A343E and A343F automatic transmissions. These
automatic transmissions are 4-speed ECT (Electronic Control Transmission).
(cid:2) For the A340E and A340F automatic transmissions that have been adopted on the 1KD-FTV and
2KD-FTV High Version engine models, the engine ECU and the transmission control ECU are separate.
The engine ECU and the transmission control ECU exchange information via CAN (Controller Area
Network) communication.
(cid:2) The automatic transmissions and their applications are indicated below.

(cid:2) The torque converter clutch supports lock-up clutch control, thus improving the fuel economy.

(cid:1)OIL PUMP
The oil pump is combined with the torque converter, lubricates the planetary gear units and supplies operating
pressure to the hydraulic control system. The drive gear of the oil pump is continually driven by the engine
via the torque converter pump impeller. The pump has sufficient capacity to supply the necessary fluid
pressure throughout all speed ranges, as well as in reverse.

2. Solenoid Valve SLT
In order to provide a hydraulic pressure that is proportion to current that flows to the solenoid coil, the

ECU (Transmission Control ECU).

CHASSIS - A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-23
4. Construction and Operation
Transmission Control Switch and Neutral Start Switch
The engine & ECT ECU (Transmission Control ECU) uses these switches to detect the shift position.
(cid:2) The neutral start switch sends the R, D, 2, L and NSW position signals to the engine & ECT ECU
(Transmission Control ECU). It also sends signals for the shift indicator light (P, R, N, 2 and L) in the
combination meter (only for optitron type).
(cid:2) The transmission control switch is located in the shift lock ECU. This switch sends the 3rd signal to the
engine & ECT ECU (Transmission Control ECU). It also sends signals for the shift position indicator
light (D and 3rd) in the combination meter.
(cid:3) Wiring Diagram (cid:4)

Input Speed Sensor and Output Speed Sensor
These automatic transmissions use an input speed sensor (for NCO signal) and output speed sensor (for SP2
signal). Thus, the engine & ECT ECU (Transmission Control ECU) can detect the timing of the shifting of the
gears and appropriately control the engine torque and hydraulic pressure in response to the various conditions.
These speed sensors are the pick-up coil type.
(cid:2) The input speed sensor detects the input speed of the transmission. The O/D direct clutch drum is used
as the timing rotor for this sensor.
(cid:2) The output speed sensor detects the speed of the output shaft.

CH-24 CHASSIS - A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
5. Line Pressure Control
In order to obtain a predetermined line pressure characteristic according to the each sensor signal, the engine
& ECT ECU (Transmission Control ECU) activates the solenoid valve SLT to regulate the throttle pressure.
This makes it possible for the primary regulator valve to precisely and minutely control the line pressure in
accordance with the engine output, and thus realize smoother shift characteristics.

CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-25
6. Engine Torque Control (for A340E and A340F)
Engagement of the clutches and brakes of the planetary gear unit in the transmission is controlled smoothly
by momentarily reducing the fuel injection volume when gears are shifted up or down in the transmission.
When the transmission control ECU judges a gear shift timing according to signals, it activates the shift
control solenoid valves to perform gear shifting. When the gear shifting starts, the transmission control ECU
reduces the fuel injection volume to reduce the engine torque. As a result, engagement force of the clutches
and brakes of the planetary gear units is weakened and the gear change is performed smoothly.

CH-26 CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
7. Engine Torque Control (for A343E and A343F)
Engagement of the clutches and brakes of the planetary gear unit in the transmission is controlled smoothly
by momentarily retarding the engine ignition timing when gears are shifted up or down in the transmission.
When the engine & ECT ECU judges a gear shift timing according to signals, it activates the shift control
solenoid valves to perform gear shifting. When the gear shifting starts, the engine & ECT ECU retards the
engine ignition timing to reduce the engine torque. As a result, engagement force of the clutches and brakes
of the planetary gear units is weakened and the gear change is performed smoothly.

CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-27
8. Diagnosis
(cid:2) When the engine & ECT ECU (Transmission Control ECU) detects a malfunction, the engine & ECT ECU
(Transmission Control ECU) makes a diagnosis and memorizes the failed section.
Furthermore, the check engine warning light in the combination meter illuminates to inform the driver.
(cid:2) At the same time, the DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) are stored in memory.
(cid:2) The DTCs can be read by connecting a intelligent tester II to DLC3.
(cid:2) The DTCs can be read from number of the blinking of the check engine warning light by connecting the
SST (09843-18040) to the Tc and CG terminals of the DLC3. This function is only available on models
for Philippines and Malaysia.
For details, see the Hilux Repair Manual.
9. Fail Safe
This function minimizes the loss of operability when any abnormality occurs in each sensor or solenoid.
CH
(cid:3) Fail-Safe Control List (cid:4)
Malfunction Part Function
During an output speed sensor malfunction, shift control is effected
Output Speed Sensor (SP2)
through the input speed sensor signal (NC0) or engine speed signal (NE).
During a malfunction of ATF temp. sensor No. 1, the system effects control
ATF Temp. Sensor No. 1
by fixing the ATF temp. at 80(cid:6)C (176(cid:6)F).
Solenoid Valve The current to the failed solenoid valve is cut off and control is effected by
No. 1 and No. 2 operating the other solenoid valves with normal operation.
During a solenoid valve SL malfunction, the current to the solenoid valve
Solenoid Valve SL is stopped. Because this stops lock-up control, and fuel economy
decreases.
During a solenoid valve SLT malfunction, the current to the solenoid valve
is stopped. Because this stops line pressure control, the shift shock
Solenoid Valve SLT
increases. However, shifting is effected through normal clutch pressure
control.

CH-28 CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
(cid:1)SHIFT CONTROL MECHANISM
1. General
(cid:2) A gate type shift lever is used. With the gate type, the shift lever button and the overdrive switch of the
straight type shift lever have been discontinued. Similar functions are achieved through a single-shift
operation (fore-aft and side-to-side).
(cid:2) This shift lever mainly consists of the shift lock ECU, shift lever plate, and shift lever sub-assembly.
(cid:2) Shift lock ECU has a built-in transmission control switch, P detection switch, and shift lock solenoid.
(cid:2) A shift lock system consists of the key interlock device and shift lock mechanism.

CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-29
2. Shift Lock System
General
(cid:2) The key interlock device prevents the key from being pulled out after the ignition switch is turned OFF,
unless the shift lever is moved to the P position. Thus, the driver is urged to park the vehicle in the P
position.
(cid:2) The shift lock mechanism prevents the shift lever from being shifted to a position other than the P
position, unless the ignition switch is ON and the brake pedal is pressed. It prevents the vehicle from
starting off suddenly against the wish of the driver.

CH-30 CHASSIS – A340E, A340F, A343E AND A343F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
Key Interlock Solenoid
The activation of the key interlock solenoid that Key Interlock Solenoid
is mounted on the upper column bracket moves
the lock pin to restrict the movement of the key
cylinder. Therefore, if the shift lever is shifted to
any position other than “P”, the ignition key
cannot be moved from “ACC” to the “LOCK”
position.
271CH25
System Operation
(cid:2) The shift lock ECU uses the P detection switch to detect the shift lever position, and receives the inputs
of the stop light switch and ignition switch signals. Upon receiving these signals, the shift lock ECU turns
ON the key interlock solenoid and the shift lock solenoid in order to release the key interlock and shift
lock.
(cid:2) A shift lock override button, which manually overrides the shift lock mechanism, is provided.

(cid:2) The new Hilux has adopted the A750E (for 2WD model) and A750F (for 4WD model) automatic
transmissions on the 1GR-FE engine models. These automatic transmissions are 5-speed Super ECT
(Electronic Controlled Transmission) to improve the vehicle’s fuel economy and driving performance.
(cid:2) The only difference between the A750E and A750F is in the extension housing and output shaft.
(cid:2) A low-viscosity type transmission fluid (TOYOTA ATF WS) has been adopted.

CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-33
(cid:1)ATF
1. General
(cid:2) The TOYOTA ATF WS has been adopted to reduce the resistance of the ATF and improve fuel economy
by reducing its viscosity in the practical operating range. At the high-temperature end, its viscosity is the
same as that of the TOYOTA ATF Type T-IV, which ensures the durability of the automatic transmission.
(cid:2) There is no interchangeability between the TOYOTA ATF WS and other types of ATFs (TOYOTA ATF
Type T-IV, D-II).
: TOYOTA ATF Type T-IV
Viscosity Reduced Viscosity : TOYOTA ATF WS
High
CH
Temperature
High
259LSK03
Service Tip
If a vehicle with a transmission filled with TOYOTA ATF WS is replenished with another type of
ATF, the vehicle might not start off at extremely low temperatures. However, it will start off after it
has been warmed up for a few minutes.
2. ATF Filling Procedures
The ATF filling procedure has been changed in order to improve the accuracy of the ATF level when being
repaired or replaced. As a result, the oil filler tube and the oil level gauge used in the conventional automatic
transmission have been discontinued, achieving a maintenance-free ATF level.
(cid:2) This mechanism consists of a refill plug, overflow plug, ATF temp. sensor No. 2, and ATF temp. warning light.
Refill Plug
Proper
Level
Overflow Plug 271CH60

CH-34 CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
Service Tip
ATF Filling procedures using SST (09843-18040)
When a large amount of ATF needs to be filled (i. e. after removal and installation of oil pan or torque
converter), perform the procedure from step 1. When the small amount of ATF is required (i. e.
removal and installation of oil cooler tube, repair of oil leak), perform the procedure from step 7.
1)Raise the vehicle keeping it level.
2)Remove the refill plug and overflow plug.
3)Fill ATF from the refill plug until it overflows from the overflow plug.
(cid:2) The fluid to fill should be TOYOTA ATF WS.
4)Install the overflow plug.
5)Fill the specified amount of ATF determined by the procedure and install the refill plug.
(cid:2) Example
Procedure Amount Liters (US qts, Imp.qts)
Removal and installation of an oil pan (including oil drainage) 1.7 (1.80, 1.50)
Removal and installation of a transmission valve body 4.3 (4.54, 3.78)
Replacement of a torque converter 5.4 (5.70, 4.75)
6)Lower the vehicle down.
7)Use the SST (09843-18040) to short the terminals TC and CG of the DLC3 connector.
8)Start the engine and allow it to idle.
(cid:2) A/C switch must be turned off.
9)Move the shift lever slowly through each of the positions from the P to the L position, and move
it back to the P position.
10) Move the shift lever to the D position, and quickly move then back and forth between N and D
(once per less than 1.5 seconds) for at least six seconds. This will activate the oil temperature
detection mode.
Standard: The ATF temp. warning light remains illuminated for 2 seconds and it goes off.
11) Return the shift lever to the P position and disconnect the terminal TC.
12) Idle the engine to raise the oil temperature.
13) Immediately after the ATF temp. warning light turns on, lift the vehicle up.
(cid:2) The ATF temp. warning light will indicate the ATF temp. according to the following table.
(Insert table here)
Less than optimized Optimized tempera- More than optimized
ATF Temp.
temperature ture temperature
A/T OIL TEMP
Turn OFF Turn ON Blinking
Warning Light
14) Remove the overflow plug and adjust oil quantity.
(cid:2) If the ATF overflows, go to step 17, and if the ATF does not overflow, go to step 15.
15) Remove the refill plug.
16) Fill ATF to the refill plug until it flows out from the overflow plug.
17) When the ATF flow slows to a trickle, install the overflow plug through the new gasket.
18) Install the refill plug (only if the refill plug has been removed).
19) Lower the vehicle down.
20) Turn the ignition switch OFF to stop the engine.
For details about the ATF Filling procedures, see the Hilux Repair Manual.

CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-41
4. Centrifugal Fluid Pressure Canceling Mechanism
There are two reasons for improving the conventional clutch mechanism:
(cid:2) To prevent the generation of pressure by centrifugal force applied to the fluid in the piston fluid pressure
chamber (hereafter referred to as “chamber A”) when the clutch is released, a check ball is provided.
Therefore, before the clutch could be subsequently applied, it took time to fill chamber A.
(cid:2) During shifting, in addition to the original clutch pressure that is controlled by the valve body, centrifugal
pressure acts on the fluid in the chamber A exerting increased pressure depending upon revolution
fluctuations.
To address these two needs for improvement, a canceling fluid pressure chamber (hereafter referred to as
“chamber B”) has been provided opposite chamber A.
C Clutch
Piston 1
CH
Chamber B
Chamber A
271CH40
By utilizing the lubrication fluid such as that of the shaft, the same amount of centrifugal force is applied,
thus canceling the centrifugal force that is applied to the piston itself. Accordingly, it is not necessary to
discharge the fluid through the use of a check ball, and a highly responsive and smooth shifting characteristic
has been achieved.

Lock-up Timing Control based on signals from each sensor and engages or disengages the lock-up
clutch.
When the shift lever is shifted from “N” to “D” position, the gear is
“N” to “D” Squat Control
temporarily shifted to 2nd and then to 1st to reduce vehicle squat.
Based on the signals from various sensors, the Engine & ECT ECU
AI (Artificial Intelligence)
determines the road conditions and the intention of the driver. Thus, the shift
-SHIFT
pattern is automatically regulated to an optimal level, thus improving
(See Page CH-53)
drivability.
When the Engine & ECT ECU detects a malfunction, the Engine & ECT
ECU makes a diagnosis and memorizes the failed section.
DDiiaaggnnoossiiss
All the DTC (Diagnostic Trouble Code) have been made correspond to the
SAE controlled codes.
Even if a malfunction is detected in the sensors or solenoids, the Engine &
Fail-safe ECT ECU effects fail-safe control to prevent the vehicle’s drivability from
being affected significantly.

CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-49
4. Construction and Operation of Main Components
ATF Temp. Sensor No. 1 and No. 2
(cid:2) ATF temp. sensor No. 1 (THO1) is used for hydraulic pressure control. This sensor is used for revision
of clutches and brakes pressure to keep smooth shift quality every time.
(cid:2) ATF temp. sensor No. 2 (THO2) is used for shifting of the shift timing control of ECT when the ATF
temp. is high. However, on the 4WD model, it is also used for the ATF temp. warning light.
ATF Temperature Sensor No. 1 (THO1)
Front
CH
ATF Temperature Sensor No. 2 (THO2)

Transmission Control Switch and Park/Neutral Position Switch
The Engine & ECT ECU uses these switches to detect the shift position.
(cid:2) The neutral start switch sends the P, R, N, D, 3, 2, and NSW position signals to the Engine & ECT ECU.
It also sends signals for the shift indicator light (P, R, N, and 3rd) in the combination meter (only for
optitron type).
(cid:2) The transmission control switch is located in the shift lock ECU. This switch sends the 4th and L signals
to the Engine & ECT ECU. It also sends signals for the shift position indicator light (D, 4th, 2nd, and
L) in the combination meter.

CH-50 CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
Input Speed Sensor and Output Speed Sensor
The A750E and A750F automatic transmissions use an input speed sensor (for NT signal) and output speed
sensor (for SP2 signal). Thus, the Engine & ECT ECU can detect the timing of the shifting of the gears and
appropriately control the engine torque and hydraulic pressure in response to the various conditions.
These speed sensors are the pick-up coil type.
(cid:2) The input speed sensor detects the input speed of the transmission. The clutch drum is used as the timing
rotor for this sensor.
(cid:2) The output speed sensor detects the speed of the output shaft. The parking gear on the rear planetary gear
is used as the timing rotor for this sensor.

5. Clutch Pressure Control
Clutch to Clutch Pressure Control
This control is used for shifting from the 4th to 5th gear and from the 5th to 4th gear.
Actuates solenoid valves SL1 and SL2 in accordance with the signals from the Engine & ECT ECU, and
guides this output pressure directly to the control valves B and C in order to regulate the line pressure that
1 1
acts on the B brake and C clutch.
1 1
As a result, high response and excellent shift characteristics have been realized.

CH-52 CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
6. Line Pressure Optimal Control
Through the use of the solenoid valve SLT, the line pressure is optimally controlled in accordance with the
engine torque information, as well as with the internal operating conditions of the toque converter and the
transmission.
Accordingly, the line pressure can be controlled minutely in accordance with the engine output, traveling
condition, and the ATF temp., thus realizing smooth shift characteristics and optimizing the workload in the
oil pump.

automatically switch the optimal shift pattern. As a result, a comfortable ride has been achieved.

CH-54 CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS
Road Condition Support Control
Under Road Condition Support Control, the Engine & ECT ECU identifies throttle valve opening angle and
the vehicle speed whether the vehicle is being driven uphill or downhill.
To achieve an optimal drive force while driving uphill, this control prevents the transmission from
upshifting to the 4th or 5th gear. To achieve an optimal engine brake effect while driving downhill, this
control automatically downshifts the transmission to the 4th or 3rd gear.
5th
5th 4th 5th 3rd 5th 4th (Brake Operating) without Control
with Control
5th 4th 3rd 4th 3rd 4th 5th
229LC183
Driver’s Intention Support Control
Estimates the driver’s intention based on the accelerator operation and vehicle condition to switch to a shift
pattern that is well-suited to each driver, without the need to operate the shift pattern select switch.

CHASSIS – A750E AND A750F AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSIONS CH-55
8.Diagnosis
(cid:2) When the Engine & ECT ECU detected a malfunction, the Engine & ECT ECU makes a diagnosis and
memorizes the failed section. Furthermore, the check engine warning light in the combination meter
illuminates or blinks to inform the driver.
(cid:2) At the same time, the DTCs (Diagnosis Trouble Codes) are stored in memory. The DTCs can be read by
connecting a intelligent tester-II to DLC3.
For details, see the Hilux Repair Manual.
9.Fail Safe
This function minimizes the loss of operability when any abnormality occurs in each sensor or solenoid.
(cid:3) Fail-Safe Control List (cid:4)
Malfunction Part Function
CH
During an output speed sensor malfunction, shift control is effected
Output Speed Sensor (SP2)
through the input speed sensor signal (SPD).
During an ATF temp. sensor No. 1 malfunction, 5th up-shift and lock-up
ATF Temp. Sensor No. 1
clutch control are prohibited.
The current to the failed solenoid valve is cut off and control is effected by
Solenoid Valve operating the other solenoid valves with normal operation.
S1, S2 and SR Shift control is effected depending on the failed solenoid as described in
the table on the next page.
Solenoid Valve During a solenoid valve SL1 or SL2 malfunction, 5th up-shift is
SL1 and SL2 prohibited.
During a solenoid valve SLU malfunction, the current to the solenoid
Solenoid Valve SLU valve is stopped. Because this stops lock-up control and lock-up control,
and fuel economy decreases.
During a solenoid valve SLT malfunction, the current to the solenoid valve
is stopped. Because this stops line pressure optimal control, the shift shock
Solenoid Valve SLT
increases. However, shifting is effected through normal clutch pressure
control.

A gate type shift lever is used in conjunction with the installation of the 5-speed automatic transmission. With
the gate type, the shift lever button and the overdrive switch of the straight type shift lever have been
discontinued. Similar functions are achieved through a single-shift operation (fore-aft and side-to-side). For
details, refer to A340E, A340F, A343E and A343F automatic transmissions on page CH-28.

(cid:2) The new Hilux uses manual operation type 4WD system.

CH-60 CHASSIS – TRANSFER
(cid:1)PLANETARY GEAR
1. General
(cid:2) The planetary sun gear integrates with the transfer input shaft.
(cid:2) 4 planetary pinion gears are fitted to the planetary carrier. Each pinion gear shaft is fixed to the planetary
carrier. A planetary spline piece is fitted to the rear of the planetary carrier and internal gear teeth of the
planetary spline piece can be engaged with the external teeth of the low and high clutch sleeve. At the rear
of the transfer input shaft, a high and low clutch sleeve is installed via the synchromesh mechanism on
the output shaft.
(cid:2) The planetary ring gear is fixed to the transfer case and internal teeth are meshed with the planetary pinion
gear.
Planetary Ring Gear

Planetary Sun Gear
Planetary Pinion Gear
232CH53

CHASSIS – TRANSFER CH-61
2. H2/H4 Position
In the H2/H4 position, the splines at the rear of the transfer input shaft mesh with the internal gear teeth of
the high and low clutch sleeve.
Also, the high and low clutch sleeve is meshed to the rear output shaft via the high and low clutch hub. Thus,
the rotation of the input shaft is transmitted to the high and low clutch sleeve, high and low clutch hub, and
to the rear output shaft.

233CH21
3. L4 Position
In the L4 position, the external teeth of the high and low clutch sleeve are meshed with the planetary spline
piece.
Thus, the rotation of the input shaft is transmitted in a reduced form to the planetary sun gear, planetary pinion
gear, planetary pinion gear shaft, planetary carrier, planetary spline piece, high and low clutch sleeve, high
and low clutch hub, and rear output shaft.

(cid:1)4WD SYSTEM
1. General
(cid:2) In the 4WD system of the new Hilux, the VF2A transfer switches to H2, H4, or L4 when the driver
manually operates the 4WD shift lever.
(cid:2) On a model equipped with a front differential with A.D.D. (Automatic Disconnecting Differential) shift
actuator, when the driver operates the shift lever to 4WD or 2WD, the 4WD control ECU operates the
A.D.D. shift actuator in order to lock or free the engagement of the front drive shaft and the front
differential.

General
On a model equipped with a front differential with A.D.D. shift actuator, the 4WD detection switch provided
in the transfer detects that the driver has operated the 4WD shift lever to the 4WD (H2 (cid:2) H4) or 2WD (H4

engagement of

CH-66 CHASSIS – TRANSFER
H2 (cid:2) H4 (4WD)
(cid:2) When the 4WD shift lever turns to H4 position, this signal is input into the 4WD control ECU, and the
4WD control ECU actuates the A.D.D. shift motor.
(cid:2) The operation of the A.D.D. shift motor causes the A.D.D. shift fork and sleeve to move towards the front
differential, thus engaging the front drive shaft and the front differential. As a result, the drive force from
the transfer is transmitted to the front wheels.

(cid:2) When the 4WD shift lever turns to H2 position, this signal is input into the 4WD control ECU, and the
4WD control ECU actuates the A.D.D. shift motor.
(cid:2) The operation of the A.D.D. shift motor causes the A.D.D. shift fork and sleeve to move towards the front
wheels, thus freeing the engagement of the front drive shaft and the front differential. This prevents the
front differential and the front propeller shaft from being rotated by the front wheels while the vehicle
is being driven.

Actuator Portion
The brake actuator consists of 6 two-position solenoid valves, 1 motor, 2 pumps and 2 reservoirs. The 6
two-position solenoid valves consists of 3 pressure holding valves [(1), (2), (3)] and 3 pressure reduction
valves [(4), (5), (6)].

CHASSIS – BRAKE CH-81
5. System Operation
ABS Operation
Based on the signals received from the 4 wheel speed sensors, the skid control ECU calculates each wheel
speed and deceleration, and checks wheel slipping condition. According to the slipping condition, the ECU
controls the pressure holding valve and pressure reduction valve in order to adjust the fluid pressure of each
wheel cylinder in the following 3 modes: pressure reduction, pressure holding, and pressure increase modes.

CH-82 CHASSIS – BRAKE
6. Skid Control ECU
Initial Check
After the ignition switch is turned ON, and the vehicle attains an approximate speed of 6 km/h (4 mph) or
more only at first time, the skid control ECU performs the initial check.
The function of each solenoid valve and pump motor in the brake actuator are check in order.
Self-Diagnosis
(cid:2) If the skid control ECU detects a malfunction in the ABS system, the ABS warning light that corresponds
to the function in which the malfunction has been detected indicates or lights up, indicated in the table
below, to alert the driver of the malfunction.
(cid:5): Light ON —: Light OFF
Item ABS Skid Control ECU
ABS Warning Light (cid:5) (cid:5)
(cid:2) At the same time, the DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) are stored in memory. The DTCs can be read
by connecting the SST (09843-18040) between the Tc and CG terminals of DLC3 and observing the
blinks of the ABS warning light (2-digit code), or by connecting an intelligent tester II (5-digit code).
(cid:2) This system has a sensor signal check (test mode) function. This check function performs speed sensor
check. This function is activated by connecting an intelligent tester II.
(cid:2) If the skid control ECU detects a malfunction during a sensor check, it stores the DTC in its memory.
This DTC can be read during a sensor signal check operation by connecting the Tc and CG terminals of
the DLC3 and observing the blinking of the ABS warning light, or on an intelligent tester II.
(cid:2) For details concerning the above, see the Hilux Repair Manual.
Fail-Safe
If a malfunction occurs in the signal system of the ABS system, the skid control ECU disables the ABS
control.

CH-84 CHASSIS – BRAKE
(cid:1)STEERING GEAR
(cid:2) A low friction rack guide is used in order to achieve a steering operation feeling at the center position.
(cid:2) Internal-external tube bushings are used to mount the steering gear on the vehicle. Accordingly, the rigidity
in the left and right directions has been optimized and the vertical rigidity and the rigidity in the forward
and rearward directions have been enhanced to optimize compliance steer and eliminate vibration from
the ground.
(cid:2) On the 4WD model, a check valve is provided at the high pressure port in order to reduce the extent of
shocks that are applied from the road surface.

CHASSIS – BRAKE CH-85
(cid:1)TILT MECHANISM
The tilt mechanism mainly consists of a tilt lever, steering column tube attachment, brakeaway bracket, tilt
lever lock bolt and tilt steering stoppers.
When the tilt mechanism is in its locked state, the tilt lever at B position causes the cam of the tilt steering
stoppers to tighten the steering column tube attachment.
When the tilt mechanism is in its free state, the tilt lever at A position causes the cam of the tilt steering
stoppers to loosen the steering column tube attachment.

CH-86 CHASSIS – BRAKE
(cid:1)ENERGY ABSORBING MECHANISM
1. General
The energy absorbing mechanism in the steering column consists of a lower bracket, breakaway bracket,
intermediate shaft and energy absorbing plate. The steering column is mounted onto the instrument panel
reinforcement via a lower bracket and breakaway bracket. The steering column and the steering gear box are
connected with a contractile intermediate shaft.
2. Operation
(cid:2) When the steering gear box moves during a collision (primary collision), the intermediate shaft contracts,
thus helps reduce the chance that the steering column and the steering wheel protrude into the cabin.
(cid:2) When an impact is transmitted to the steering wheel in a collision (secondary collision), the steering wheel
and the driver airbag help absorb the impact. In addition, the breakaway bracket and the lower bracket
separate, causing the entire steering column to move forward.
At this time, the energy absorbing plate becomes deformed to help absorbing the impact of the secondary
collision.

EG-2 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
ENGINE
1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) The 1TR-FE and 2TR-FE engines that have been adopted on the new Hilux are newly developed, inline
4-cylinder, 2.0/2.7-liter, 16-valve DOHC engines.
(cid:2) These engines have been adopted the DIS (Direct Ignition System), ETCS-i (Electronic Throttle Control
System-intelligent), VVT-i (Variable Valve Timing-intelligent) system and an Air Injection system*1. It
has been developed to realize high performance, quietness, fuel economy and clean emission.
(cid:2) The 2TR-FE engine is available for use with leaded*2 or unleaded*3 gasoline, depending on the
destination.
(cid:2) The basic construction and operation of both the 1TR-FE and 2TR-FE engines (for leaded and unleaded
gasoline) are the same. For details, refer to “Major Difference” on page EG-4.
*1: for Thai, Australian, and Central and South American Models
*2: TGN16L-TRMDK, TGN16L-CRMDK, TGN16L-PRMDK and TGN26L-PRMDK
*3: Other Models

EG-6 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
(cid:1)ENGINE PROPER
1. Cylinder Head Cover
(cid:2) The cylinder head cover is made of plastic to reduce weight and noise.
(cid:2) Acrylic rubber, which excels in heat resistance and reliability, is used for the cylinder head cover gasket.
(cid:2) Baffle plates made of plastic are provided inside the cylinder head cover to reduce the consumption of
engine oil through blow-by gas. The baffle plates are welded onto the cylinder head cover, and cannot be
removed.
Baffle Plate
Cylinder Head Cover
Cylinder Head
Cover Gasket
271EG05
2. Cylinder Head Gasket
(cid:2) A steel-laminate type cylinder head gasket has been adopted.
(cid:2) A shim is provided around the cylinder bore to increase the sealing surface, thus improving the sealing
performance and durability.
A A
Shim
A – A Cross Section
271EG06

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-7
3. Cylinder Head
(cid:2) The cylinder head, which is made of aluminum alloy, contains a pentroof-type combustion chamber. The
spark plug has been located in the center of the combustion chamber in order to improve the engine’s
anti-knocking performance.
(cid:2) The angle of the intake and exhaust valves is narrowed and set at 22.85(cid:5) to permit a compact cylinder head.
(cid:2) A cross-flow intake and exhaust layout is used to improve intake and exhaust efficiency.
(cid:2) A taper squish combustion chamber is used to improve anti-knocking performance and intake efficiency.
In addition, engine performance and fuel economy have been improved.
(cid:2) The cylinder head bolt employs plastic region tightening bolts.
(cid:2) Long nozzle type injectors are installed in the cylinder head to reduce the distance from injector to intake EG
valve, thus it prevents the fuel from adhering to the intake port walls, and reduce exhaust emissions.
(cid:2) The routing of the water jacket in the cylinder head is optimized to achieve higher cooling performance.
(cid:2) An air injection port, which directs the air from the air pump to the exhaust port of each cylinder, has been
provided on top of the exhaust port on the model with the air injection system.

EG-8 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
4. Cylinder Block
(cid:2) The cylinder block is made of cast iron, with the rib shape of each part optimized to increase reigidity while
reducing vibration and noise.
(cid:2) Water passages have been provided between the cylinder bores of the 2TR-FE engine. By allowing the
engine coolant to flow between the cylinder bores, this construction enables the temperature of the
cylinder walls to be kept uniform.

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-9
5. Balance Shaft (for 2TR-FE Engine)
(cid:2) For in-line 4 cylinder engines, the main cause of vibration is imbalanced inertial force of reciprocating
parts such as the pistons and connecting rods. The 2TR-FE engine vibration has been reduced by using
2 balance shafts to cancel the imbalanced inertial force, thereby reducing engine noise (booming noise).
(cid:2) 2 balance shafts are built into the cylinder block. Using a chain and idler gear, both balance shafts rotate
at twice the speed of the crankshaft with each balance shaft rotating in the opposite direction of the other.
(cid:2) The chain tensioner uses a spring and oil pressure to maintain proper chain tension at all times. A ratchet
type non-return mechanism is also included.

EG-10 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
6. Piston
(cid:2) The piston is made of aluminum alloy.
(cid:2) The piston head portion uses a taper squish shape to accomplish fuel combustion efficiency.
(cid:2) The piston skirt has been coated with resin to reduce the friction loss.
(cid:2) The groove of the top ring has been coated with anodic oxide to improve wear resistance and rust
resistance.
(cid:2) On the leaded gasoline type 2TR-FE engine, a PVD (Physical Vapor Deposition) coating has been applied
on the surface of the No. 1 compression ring, and the surface of the No. 2 compression ring has been
chrome-plated to improve their wear resistance.

7. Connecting Rod and Connecting Rod Bearing
(cid:2) Connecting rods that have been forged for high strength are used for weight reduction.
(cid:2) Knock pins are used at the mating surfaces of the bearing caps of the connecting rod to minimize the
shifting of the bearing caps during assembly.
(cid:2) Plastic region tightening bolts are used.
(cid:2) The lining surface of the connecting rod bearing has been micro-grooved to realize an optimal amount of
oil clearance. As a result, cold-engine cranking performance has been improved and engine vibrations
have been reduced.

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-11
8. Crankshaft and Crankshaft Bearing
(cid:2) The crankshaft has 5 journals and 8 balance weights.
(cid:2) All pin and journal fillets are roll-finished to maintain adequate strength.
(cid:2) The crankshaft bearing is made of aluminum alloy.
(cid:2) The crankshaft bearing caps are tightened using 2 plastic-region tightening bolts for each journal.
(cid:2) Similar to the connecting rod bearings, the lining surface of the crankshaft bearings has been
micro-grooved to realize an optimal amount of oil clearance. As a result, cold-engine cranking
performance has been improved and engine vibrations have been reduced.
(cid:2) The upper main bearing has an oil groove around its inside circumference.

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-13
(cid:1)VALVE MECHANISM
1.General
(cid:2) The 1TR-FE and 2TR-FE engines have adopted a valve actuation mechanism that uses roller rocker arms
with built-in needle bearings. This reduces the friction that occurs between the cams and the areas (roller
rocker arms) that push the valves down, thus improving fuel economy.
(cid:2) A hydraulic lash adjuster, which maintains a constant zero valve clearance through the use of oil pressure
and spring force, has been adopted.
(cid:2) The VVT-i system is used to achieve low fuel consumption, higher engine performance and reduce exhaust
emissions. For details of VVT-i control, see page EG-52.
(cid:2) On the leaded gasoline type 2TR-FE engine, the valve faces of the intake and exhaust valves have been EG
metallized to improve their wear resistance.

2. Camshaft
(cid:2) An oil passage is provided in the intake camshaft in order to supply engine oil pressure to the VVT-i
system.
(cid:2) A VVT-i controller is installed on the front of the intake camshaft to vary the timing of the intake valves.

3. Hydraulic Lash Adjuster
(cid:2) The hydraulic lash adjuster, which is located at the fulcrum of the roller rocker arm, consists primarily of
a plunger, plunger spring, check ball, and check ball spring.
(cid:2) The engine oil that is supplied by the cylinder head and the built-in spring actuate the hydraulic lash
adjuster. The oil pressure and the spring force that act on the plunger push the roller rocker arm against
the cam, in order to adjust the valve clearance that is created during the opening and closing of the valve.
This results in both improving the quietness of the engine and making valve clearance adjustments
unnecessary.

(cid:2) A high-strength roller chain with a 9.525 mm pitch has been adopted to make the engine more compact
and to ensure the reliability of the timing chain.
(cid:2) The timing chain is lubricated by timing chain oil jet.
(cid:2) The chain tensioner uses a spring and oil pressure to maintain proper chain tension at all times. The chain
tensioner suppresses noise generated by the timing chain.
(cid:2) A ratchet type non-return mechanism is used in the chain tensioner.

construction and a compact engine.
(cid:2) Piston oil jets to lubricate and cool the pistons and a timing chain oil jet to lubricate the timing chain have
been provided.
(cid:2) An oil delivery pipe has been provided to deliver engine oil to the cams and roller rocker arms in order
to ensure the lubrication of the sliding areas.

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-19
(cid:1)COOLING SYSTEM
(cid:2) The cooling system uses a pressurized forced circulation system with open air type reservoir tank.
(cid:2) A thermostat with a bypass valve is located on the water inlet housing to maintain suitable temperature
distribution in the cooling system.
(cid:2) A water pump swirl chamber and a thermostat housing have been placed in the timing chain cover to
achieve a simpler construction and a compact engine.
(cid:2) An aluminum radiator core is used for weight reduction.
(cid:2) The water bypass pipe made of plastic is used for weight reduction.
(cid:2) A 3-stage temperature-controlled coupling fan, which uses the same bimetal that is used on the
conventional model, controls the fan speed in three stages to improve cooling performance and reduce EG
cooling fan noise.
(cid:2) The TOYOTA genuine Super Long Life Coolant (SLLC) has been adopted. As a result, the maintenance
interval has been extended.

filled with LLC (red-colored), if you use SLLC (pink-colored) for the engine coolant change.

2. Throttle Body
(cid:2) A DC motor with excellent response and minimal power consumption is used for the throttle control motor.
The engine ECU performs the duty ratio control of the direction and the amperage of the current that flows
to the throttle control motor in order to regulate the opening angle of the throttle valve.
(cid:2) The link-less type throttle body has adopted and it realizes excellent throttle control.

Reduction Gears
3. Intake Manifold
(cid:2) The intake manifold has been made of plastic to reduce the weight and the amount of heat transferred from
the cylinder head. As a result, it has become possible to reduce the intake air temperature and improve the
intake volumetric efficiency.
(cid:2) This intake manifold consists of three parts, which are integrated through vibration welding.
Intake Manifold
271EG28
— REFERENCE —
Vibration Welding:
Vibration welding is a type of friction welding. In this process, one part of the work to be welded remains
stationary while vibration and pressure are applied to the other part, thus causing the joining surfaces to melt
and become welded. This method creates a joint that is stronger than other joining methods, such as those
using adhesives.

EG-24 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
(cid:1)FUEL SYSTEM
1. General
(cid:2) The injector is shaped to form a long nozzle. This shortens the distance from the injector to the intake valve,
which prevents the fuel from adhering to the intake port walls and reduces exhaust emissions. Depending
on the type of fuel that is used, a 4-hole type (for leaded gasoline) or a 12-hole type (for unleaded gasoline)
injector is provided.
(cid:2) A fuel tank made of plastic has been adopted.
(cid:2) A compact and lightweight module fuel pump with a fuel sender gauge has been adopted.
(cid:2) A quick connector is used to connect the fuel pipe with the fuel hose for excellent serviceability.

A DIS (Direct Ignition System) is used. The DIS improves the ignition timing accuracy, reduces high-voltage
loss, and enhances the overall reliability of the ignition system by eliminating the distributor. The DIS in this
engine is an independent ignition system, which has one ignition coil for each cylinder.

(cid:2) A compact and lightweight Segment Conductor type alternator that generates high amperage output in
a highly efficient manner has been adopted on standard equipment.
(cid:2) This alternator has a joined segment conductor system, in which multiple segment conductors are welded
together to form the stator. Compared to the conventional winding system, the electrical resistance is
reduced due to the shape of the segment conductors, and their arrangement helps to make the alternator
more compact.

EG-30 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
(cid:1)STARTING SYSTEM
1.General
(cid:2) A compact and lightweight PS (Planetary reduction-Segment conductor motor) type starter has been
adopted on 2TR-FE engine models. For details, see page EG-31.
(cid:2) A planetary reduction type starter with an interpolar magnet has been adopted on the 2TR-FE engine
models. For details on the interpolar magnet, see page EG-32.
(cid:2) Conventional planetary reduction type starter has been adopted on 1TR-FE engine models.

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-31
2. PS (Planetary reduction-Segment conductor motor) Starter
General
(cid:2) Because the PS type starter contains an armature that uses square-shaped conductors, and its surface
functions as a commutator, it has resulted in both improving its output torque and reducing its overall
length.
(cid:2) In place of the field coil used in the conventional type starter, the PS type starter uses two types of
permanent magnets: main magnets and interpolar magnets. The main magnets and interpolar magnets
have been efficiently arranged to increase the magnetic flux and to shorten the length of the yoke.

EG-32 ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
Construction
(cid:2) Instead of constructing the armature coil with conventional type round-shaped conductor wires, the PS
type starter uses square conductors. With this type of construction, the same conditions that are realized
by winding numerous round-shaped conductor wires can be achieved without increasing the mass. As
a result, the output torque has been increased, and the armature coil has been made more compact.
(cid:2) Because the surface of the square-shaped conductors that are used in the armature coil functions as a
commutator, the overall length of the PS type starter has been shortened.

(cid:2) Instead of the field coils used in the conventional type starter, the PS type starter has adopted two types
of permanent magnets: the main magnets and the interpolar magnets. The main and interpolar magnets
are arranged alternately inside the yoke, allowing the magnetic flux that is generated between the main
and interpolar magnets to be added to the magnetic flux that is generated by the main magnets. In addition
to increasing the amount of magnetic flux, this construction shortens the overall length of the yoke.

EG-40 ENGINE– 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES
Oxygen Sensor and Air Fuel Ratio Sensor
1) General
(cid:2) The oxygen sensor and the air fuel ratio sensor differ in output characteristics.
(cid:2) The output voltage of the oxygen sensor changes in accordance with the oxygen concentration in the
exhaust emission. The engine ECU uses this output voltage to determine whether the present air-fuel
ratio is richer or leaner than the stoichiometric air-fuel ratio.
(cid:2) Approximately 0.4V is constantly applied to the air-fuel ratio sensor, which outputs an amperage that
varies in accordance with the oxygen concentration in the exhaust emission. The engine ECU converts
the changes in the output amperage into voltage in order to linearly detect the present air-fuel ratio.

ENGINE – 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-41
2) Construction
(cid:1) The basic construction of the oxygen sensor and the air-fuel ratio sensor is the same. However, they
are divided into the cup type and the planar type, according to the different types of heater construction
that are used.
(cid:1) The cup type sensor contains a sensor element that surrounds a heater.
(cid:1) The planer type sensor uses alumina, which excels in heat conductivity and insulation, to integrate a
sensor element with a heater, thus improving the warm-up performance of the sensor.

Air Flow Meter
(cid:1) This air flow meter, which is a plug-in type, allows a portion of the intake air to flow through the detection
area. By directly measuring the mass and the flow rate of the intake air, the detection precision has been
improved and the intake air resistance has been reduced.
(cid:1) This air flow meter has a built-in intake air temperature sensor.

Crankshaft and Camshaft Position Sensors
(cid:1) The timing rotor of the crankshaft consists of 34 teeth, with 2 teeth missing. The crankshaft position
sensor outputs the crankshaft rotation signals every 10(cid:4), and the missing teeth are used to determine the
top-dead-center.
(cid:1) To detect the camshaft position, a timing rotor on the intake camshaft is used to generate 3 pulses for every
2 revolutions of the crankshaft.

ENGINE– 1TR-FE AND 2TR-FE ENGINES EG-43
Knock Sensor (Flat Type)
1) General
In the conventional type knock sensor (resonant type), a vibration plate which has the same resonance
point as the knocking frequency of the engine is built in and can detect the vibration in this frequency
band.
On the other hand, a flat type knock sensor (non-resonant type) has the ability to detect vibration in a
wider frequency band from about 6 kHz to 15 kHz, and has the following features.
(cid:1) The engine knocking frequency will change a bit depending on the engine speed. The flat type knock
sensor can detect the vibration even when the engine knocking frequency is changed. Thus the
vibration detection ability is increased compared to the conventional type knock sensor, and a more EG
precise ignition timing control is possible.
